Let's design a robust optimizer over p length N for normalized sum1.

Potential optimization methods:
A. Smooth max using log-sum-exp and L-BFGS-B in simplex via log variables (softmax). For N up to 128, we can run many starts. But nonconvex; need good initial.
B. Active-set balancing using convex (for fixed weights) linear solve via iterative "relaxation".
C. Coordinate descent with exact subproblem.

Could combine:
- Generate initial p from continuous family: exponential, cosine, sine, quadratic, random smooth.
- Improve via iterative algorithm:
   1. Compute c=p*p via direct/FFT.
   2. Identify active lags S: those with c ≥ θ * max(c) (e.g., top 10-30% or all within 1-ε of max).
   3. Compute gradient G = A_S p (derivative of sum over S c_k). Use FFT or direct.
   4. Update p to reduce max: Since we want gradient constant on support, set target λ = mean(G[p>tol]) / sum? Actually G dimension same. Projected gradient: p_i = p_i - η (G_i - λ). But G_i can be large; scale η based on p/G. A multiplicative update: p_i ← p_i / sqrt? There is known multiplicative update for minimizing max with constraints? For NORA, if c_k active, increase p_i where G_i low. Since constraint sum1, update p_i ← p_i * (λ / G_i)^ξ, normalize. This keeps nonnegative and handles scale. λ = mean(G_i) over support. ξ small (e.g.,0.1). This is mirror descent for minimizing average active c. It might be stable.
   We can use temperature: weights α_k ∝ softmax(c_k/τ) rather than binary S. Then G = A_α p computed via α weights. This balances all lags smoothly. Update p_i ← p_i * (λ/G_i)^ξ, normalize. Here λ maybe sum p / sum? To maintain sum, normalize after. This is like multiplicative weights dual ascent. Let's analyze: If G_i ≈ constant, no change. If G_i high, p_i decreases. This moves mass away from positions contributing to high convolution. Starting from family, should reduce peaks.
   Need compute G for weighted α efficiently. Let α length 2n-1 normalized sum maybe 1. G_j = Σ_k α_k (p_{k-j}+p_{j-k}) if indices valid. We can compute by convolutions:
      y1_j = Σ_k α_k p_{k-j}. This is cross-correlation of α and p. Let's map: Let p length n, α length 2n-1. Define reversed p: pr_i = p[n-1-i]. y1_j = Σ_{l=0}^{n-1} p_l α_{j+l}? Because k-j=l => k=j+l. Yes y1_j=Σ_l p_l α_{j+l}. That's convolution of p with α? np.convolve(p, α) index? (p*α)_m =Σ_l p_l α_{m-l}. Need j+l. If reverse α: ar_k=α_{L-1-k} where L=2n-1. Then (p*ar)_? Let's derive: (p * ar)_m=Σ_l p_l ar_{m-l}=Σ_l p_l α_{L-1-(m-l)}=Σ_l p_l α_{L-1-m+l}. Set j = L-1-m => m=L-1-j. Then (p*ar)_{L-1-j}=Σ_l p_l α_{j+l}= y1_j. So y1 = reverse( convolve(p, reverse(α)) ).
      y2_j = Σ_k α_k p_{j-k} = Σ_l p_l α_{j-l}. This is (α * p)? Actually (α * p)_j? α length 2n-1, p n. (p*α)_j=Σ_l p_l α_{j-l}. That's exactly y2_j. So y2=convolve(p, α) (truncated/aligned j=0..n-1). Then G=y1+y2 (with appropriate reverse). We can compute both using scipy.signal.fftconvolve or np.convolve for n small. For many α, use FFT.

Alternative implement our own projected gradient with active weights. It may be more stable and faster. Let's design a robust "balancing" algorithm.

Balancing algorithm details:
- Input initial p (length n, sum1, nonnegative).
- c = conv(p,p), F=max c.
- For iteration up to max:
   - Choose temperature τ = F * beta, beta maybe 0.05,0.1,0.2. But for initial steep, use top quantile:
       α_k = 1 if c_k > q (e.g., 0.8*F) else 0. Normalize α sum=1. Could add smoothing: α = (c_k/F)^m for active.
   - G = A_α p.
   - For support with p_i>ε_i, target λ = mean(G on support) maybe.
   - Candidate multiplicative: p_new = p_i * (λ/G_i)^βp if G_i>0 else p_i; normalize. βp maybe 0.1.
   - Evaluate F_new. If F_new < F - 1e-12, accept, reset step; if not, try smaller β (0.05,0.01) or flipped? If none, try projected gradient: p + η(λ-G) normalized. Evaluate.
   - If no improvement for many iterations, change α selection (more lags/less), add noise, or switch to L-BFGS-B.

This is heuriltic. Need guarantee return valid best at end. We can always return best found among initial/families.

Pseudo:
```
def _next_pow2(m): return 1 << (m-1).bit_length()
def _conv_direct(a,b): return np.convolve(a,b,mode='full')
def _matvec_alpha_p(p, alpha):
    n=len(p); L=2*n-1
    if n <= 512:
       a_rev=alpha[::-1]
       conv1=np.convolve(p,a_rev,mode='full')
       conv2=np.convolve(p,alpha,mode='full')
       idx=np.arange(L-1, L-n, -1)? Need verify
       t1=conv1[L-1: L-n : -1]? For n=2 L=3, L-n=1 -> slice [2:1:-1] => 2,1 good. For n=3 L=5, need indices4,3,2; L-n=2 slice [4:2:-1]=4,3 good. So `t1=conv1[L-1 : L-n : -1]` works length n? If stop inclusive? Python excludes stop, yes stops at L-n+? For n=3 stop2 exclusive gives 4,3; missing 2. Need stop=L-n-1. L=5,n=3, L-n-1=1 -> [4:1:-1]=4,3,2. General start L-1, stop L-n-1, step -1. Length: (L-1)-(L-n-1)+1=n. For n=2: L-n-1=0 -> [2:0:-1]=2,1. Good.
       t2=conv2[:n]
       return t1+t2
    else:
       size=_next_pow2(3*n-1) # need 3n-2 max+1? max index 3n-3, length 3n-2. Use 3*n.
       P=np.fft.rfft(p,size)
       A=np.fft.rfft(alpha,size)
       Ar=np.fft.rfft(alpha[::-1],size)
       conv1=np.fft.irfft(P*Ar,size)
       conv2=np.fft.irfft(P*A,size)
       t1=conv1[L-1:L-n-1:-1]
       t2=conv2[:n]
       return t1+t2
```

```
def improve_sequence(p, time_limit, rng, tau_seq, ...):
    p = normalized copy
    c, F = score_conv(p)
    best...
    patience=0
    for step in range(max_iter):
        alpha = make_alpha(c, F, temp)
        H = matvec_H(p, alpha)
        support = p > floor
        lam = mean(H[support])
        # line search over eps
        for eps in [0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5]:
            factors = (lam / np.maximum(H[support], tiny))**eps
            factors = clip(factors, 0.1, 10)
            p_new = p.copy(); p_new[support]=p[support]*factors; p_new[~support]=p[~support]*? maybe grow zeros? To keep sum, normalize all including zeros? If p zero, H maybe0; factor huge; can set zeros to floor.
            p_new = normalize_nonneg(p_new)
            Fnew = score
            if Fnew < F - 1e-12: accept eps, break
        if no accept:
            # try add noise or projected gradient
            p = normalize(p + noise*(...))
        if accepted: update; patience=0 else patience++
        if patience>...: break or random restart local
```

Let's delve into `improve_multiplicative` and parameter tuning.

Compute c and F:
```
def _convolve_full(p):
   n=len(p)
   if n < 1024: return np.convolve(p,p,mode='full')
   L=1 << int(math.log2(2*n-1 + (2*n-1 % 2==0?) -1?))
   # next pow2 >=2n-1
   L=1; while L<2*n-1: L*=2 -> helper
   F=np.fft.rfft(p,L)
   c=np.fft.irfft(F*F,L)[:2*n-1]
   return c
```
For very small n, np.convolve direct.

Alpha:
```
def _make_alpha(c, F, frac, temp, rng=None):
    if frac is not None:
       threshold = np.quantile(c, 1-frac) # top frac * (2n-1)
       alpha = np.maximum(c - threshold, 0.0)
       # add tiny to all? no
    else:
       t = F*temp
       alpha = np.exp((c - F)/t) # ≤1, top high
    alpha += eps
    alpha /= alpha.sum()
    return alpha
```
Quantile per iter O(n log n) maybe okay. Instead use threshold by fraction of max: e.g., `alpha = np.maximum(c / F - 0.7, 0)` to top 30%? But c distribution may top few. H compute:
```
def _H(p, alpha):
    n=len(p)
    L=2*n-1
    if n <= 512:
       rev=alpha[::-1]
       conv=np.convolve(p, rev, mode='full')
       # need indices L-1, L-2, ..., L-n
       idx = np.arange(L-1, L-n-1, -1)
       return conv[idx]
    else:
       size=1 << ((3*n-2 + 32? -1).bit_length()) # maybe use math
       P=np.fft.rfft(p,size)
       A=np.fft.rfft(alpha[::-1],size)
       conv=np.fft.irfft(P*A,size)
       idx=np.arange(L-1, L-n-1, -1)
       return conv[idx]
```
Check with all α: conv p with alpha_rev (all 1 length L). conv at index L-1-j? The sum over l p_l should be1 if α length enough. For j=0, index L-1=2n-2: conv[m]=Σ_l p_l α_{m-l}; α all1 for 0..2n-2; m=2n-2, l 0..n-1 => α indices 2n-2-l from n-1..2n-2 valid all -> sum p=1. Good. For j=n-1, index L-n= n-1; conv[n-1]=Σ_l p_l α_{n-1-l}; for l≤n-1, α indices n-1-l 0..n-1 valid ->sum p=1. Good.

```
def _update_multiplicative(p, H, step, floor_frac):
   support = p > 1e-14
   lam = np.mean(H[support]) if any else np.mean(H)
   Hsafe = np.maximum(H, lam*1e-8)
   factor = (lam/Hsafe)**step
   factor = np.clip(factor, 0.2, 10.0)
   # Add small uniform to avoid stagnation?
   p_new = p * factor
   p_new = (1-1e-7)*p_new + floor_frac*np.ones_like(p)? floor_frac maybe step*n? Instead set p_new = np.maximum(p_new, 1e-15)
   s=p_new.sum(); if s<1e-300: uniform
   p_new /= s
   return p_new
```

```
def _line_search_pair(c,p,i,j,maxX):
   if i==j or p[i]<=0: return 0, F
   L = np.zeros(2*n-1)
   idx = np.arange(n)
   # k=i+idx
   L[i:i+n] += -2*p[idx]  # valid since i+idx max i+n-1 ≤2n-2
   L[j:j+n] += 2*p[idx]
   Q = zeros; Q[2*i]+=1; Q[2*j]+=1; Q[i+j]-=2
   # x range [0, p[i]]
   # sample x fractions of p[i]; include endpoints
   best_x=0; best_F=F
   for frac in precomputed [0.01,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5,0.7,0.9,0.999]:
       x=frac*p[i]
       # compute max: arr = c + x*L
       # instead allocate c_x = c + x*L each time; length 2n-1. Vectorized.
       # Then max(arr + x^2*Q) but can add point adjustments.
       arr = c + x*L
       mx = arr.max()
       # adjust points: need handle if k indexes valid; Q may multiple at same k if e.g. 2i == i+j (if j=i) not, or 2i==2j no. For safety, make unique.
       for k,q in q_pairs:
           val=arr[k]+q*x*x
           if val>mx: mx=val
       if mx < best_F: best...
   # refine golden around best sample?
   if best_x>0:
      # local ternary/golden on [x_left,x_right] around neighboring samples? Since maybe not unimodal; simply do 20 random samples around best? Or Newton?
   return best_x, best_F
```

Detailed code:
```
def multiplicative_optimize(p, c, F, rng, end_time, max_iter=100000):
    n=len(p)
    best_p=p.copy(); best_F=F
    step=0.1; tau_frac=0.08; stall=0; tau_cycle=[0.15,0.08,0.04,0.02,0.01,0.005]
    iters=0
    while time.monotonic()<end_time:
        iters+=1
        tau = F*tau_cycle[tau_idx]
        alpha = _alpha_from_c(c, F, tau)
        H = _H_matrix_vector(p, alpha)
        support = p > (np.max(p)*1e-12 + 1e-300)
        if not support.any(): support = np.ones(n,bool)
        lam = float(np.mean(H[support]))
        if not np.isfinite(lam) or lam <=0: lam=1e-20
        Hsafe = np.maximum(H, lam*1e-8)
        factor = np.power(lam/Hsafe, step)
        factor = np.clip(factor, 0.05, 20.0)
        p_new = p * factor
        # add small uniform to avoid zero and maybe increase support
        p_new = (1.0 - 1e-5)*p_new + 1e-5/n
        p_new = _normalize(p_new)
        c_new = _conv_full(p_new)
        F_new = float(np.max(c_new))
        if F_new < best_F - 1e-12:
            # accept (should also be <F)
            p,p_new; c=c_new; F=F_new; best_p=...; stall=0; step=min(0.5, step*1.05)
        else:
            # if close? if F_new < F: set p? but not global
            stall += 1
            if stall > 20:
                # try smaller step or change tau
                step *= 0.5
                tau_idx=(tau_idx+1)%len
                if step < 0.005:
                    # random perturbation
                    noise = rng.normal(0, 1e-3, n)
                    p_noisy = _normalize(p * np.exp(noise))
                    c = _conv_full(p_noisy); F=float(np.max(c))
                    p=p_noisy
                    step=0.1; stall=0
                    if F < best_F: best...
            else:
                # try next tau less often
                if stall>5: tau_idx=(tau_idx+1)%len
        # If time per iter too high, max_iter not wall; wall enough.
    return best_p, c,best_F? Need c final for best? Return best_p,best_F.
```
But it returns best_p maybe not current p if current worse. At stage end, if current F > best_F, reset p=best_p for further? The caller should use returned best. But if current has better local for future? best is best by score, so use best. Simpler always keep best.
Need compute c for best? Not until next stage; can recompute in caller or return c. We can recompute at stage transitions? To save, return (best_p, best_F, c_best if current best). Hankel optimizer code:
```
def hankel_optimize(p,c,F,rng,end_time,max_iter=10000):
   n=len(p)
   best_p=...
   beta=0.5
   tau_frac=0.2
   reg=1e-6
   tau_cycle=[0.5,0.3,0.2,0.1,0.05,0.02,0.01]
   idx=0
   stall=0
   alpha_prev=None
   while time<end_time:
      alpha=_make_alpha(c,F,F*tau_cycle[idx])
      if alpha_prev is not None: alpha=(1-0.5)*alpha+0.5*alpha_prev? Or the other? Use smoothing: alpha = 0.3*new +0.7*prev to stabilize? If prev close, okay. Start new. After update, alpha_prev=alpha.
      A=_hankel_matrix(alpha,n)
      ok=False
      for r in [reg, 10*reg, 100*reg, 1e-2]:
         try: p_raw=np.linalg.solve(A+r*I, np.ones(n)); ok=True; break
         except: continue
      if not ok: p_raw=np.linalg.lstsq(A+1e-2*I,b,rcond=None)[0]
      p_raw = np.real_if_close(p_raw)
      # If raw negative, clip. If sum raw<1e-8, fallback p_new=p (or uniform mix)
      p_pos = np.clip(p_raw, 0, None)
      s=p_pos.sum()
      if s < 1e-8:
         p_new=p
      else:
         p_new=p_pos/s
         p_new=(1-beta)*p + beta*p_new; p_new=_normalize(p_new)
      c_new=_conv_full(p_new); F_new=max
      if F_new < best_F - 1e-12:
          accept; beta=min(1,beta*1.1); stall=0
      else:
          # try direct p_new without mix? if mix failed, maybe p_pos normalized (beta=1) could improve
          cand2=p_pos/s if s>...; c2... (optional, extra cost)
          if F2 < best_F: accept cand2
          else:
             stall+=1; beta*=0.7; if beta<0.05: beta=0.5; idx=(idx+1)%len; reg*=2 (cap)
             if stall>30: random perturbation, reset
   return best...
```
Building A for each iter with vstack. We can preallocate? Not needed. For n up to512, vstack lists heavy but okay. for i in range(n):
```
    A[i, :] = alpha[i:i+n]
```

Line search pair function top-level:
```
def _pair_line_search(c, p, i, j, max_fracs=None):
    n=len(p); m=2*n-1
    if i==j or p[i]<=0: return 0, float(np.max(c))
    # L vector: allocate zeros m
    L = np.zeros(m)
    # Add -2p to L[i:i+n]
    L[i:i+n] -= 2*p
    L[j:j+n] += 2*p
    # Q points
    qpoints=[]
    qpoints.append((2*i,1)); qpoints.append((2*j,1)); qpoints.append((i+j,-2))
    # sample fractions
    base = p[i]
    fracs = [0.0, 0.001,0.01,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5,0.7,0.9,0.999]
    # equalization
    if p[i] >= p[j]:
       xe=0.5*(p[i]-p[j])
       if 0<xe<base: fracs.append(xe/base)
       # p_j fraction? 
    else:
       # if moving from smaller, maybe move until equal? donor smaller, recipient larger; line search can reduce pair product by moving all donor? Actually moving from smaller to larger increases product? If pair product for k=i+j, moving from smaller to larger increases? Let donor i smaller, L_k=-2p_j+2p_i negative? If p_i<p_j, donor i smaller, L_k negative (decreases c_k) but Q negative; moving mass to larger may reduce? Example p=(1,2), move 1 to 2: new (0,3) product0 from product2; yes reduces. Equalization not relevant; moving all donor to recipient reduces (increases difference but donor zero). So just sample.
```

```
def normalize_sequence(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    a=np.clip(a,0,None)
    s=a.sum()
    if not finite or s<=0: return np.ones(n)/n
    return a/s
```

```
def _initial_starts(n, rng, max_starts=12):
    starts=[]
    x=np.linspace(0.1,0.9,n)? For n=1 use [0]
    if n==1: return [np.ones(1)]
    # uniform + noise
    starts.append(_normalize(np.ones(n)))
    starts.append(_normalize(1.0 + 0.2*rng.random(n)))
    # exp
    for alpha in [0.2,0.5,1.0,1.8,3.0,5.0]:
        p=np.exp(-alpha * np.arange(n) / n)
        starts.append(_normalize(p))
        starts.append(_normalize(p[::-1]))? same score but can help random; maybe skip reverse (score same, but optimizer random breaks symmetry; reverse same start).
    # power decay
    for a in [0.3,0.7,1.0,2.0,4.0]:
       p=(1 - np.arange(n)/max(1,n-1))**a
       starts.append(_normalize(p))
    # trig
    for a in [0.0,0.3,0.6,0.9,1.2]:
       for b in [-0.3,-0.1,0.0,0.1,0.3]:
          f=1.0 + a*np.cos(np.pi*x) + b*np.sin(np.pi*x)
          if np.min(f) > -1e-8:
             starts.append(_normalize(f))
    # quadratic/cubic random? 
    for _ in range(3):
       coeffs = rng.normal(0,0.3,4)
       f=1.0 + coeffs[0]*x + coeffs[1]*(x-0.5) + coeffs[2]*(x-0.5)**2 + coeffs[3]*(x-0.5)**3
       if min> -0.1: starts.append(_normalize(f))
    # gaussians
    for mu in [0.15,0.3,0.5,0.7]:
       for sigma in [0.1,0.2,0.4]:
          f=np.exp(-((x-mu)/sigma)**2)
          # add small uniform to support endpoints
          f = 0.8*f + 0.2
          starts.append(_normalize(f))
    # random low freq
    for _ in range(4):
       f=np.ones(n)
       for k in range(1,4):
          amp = rng.uniform(0.1,0.6) / k
          phase = rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi)
          f += amp * np.cos(2*np.pi*k*x/n? x in [0,1]) + phase? Actually cos(2π k x + phase)
       # also sin? 
       if min f > -0.5: clip and normalize
    # parent
    par = _parent_resample(n,rng)
    if par is not None: starts.append(par)
    # trim max_starts maybe select diverse
    if len(starts)>max_starts:
       # score initial? This could be costly for many N. We can just pick first max_starts or sample random indices.
       idx = rng.choice(len(starts), max_starts, replace=False); starts=[starts[i] for i in idx]
    return starts
```

`_make_alpha`: if c contains tiny negatives? We clip c before. Use F = max(c). If F <= 0, return uniform.
```
def _make_alpha(c, F, tau):
   L=c.size
   if F <= 0: return np.full(L,1.0/L)
   t = max(tau, F*1e-12) # tau passed absolute maybe F*frac
   w = np.exp((c - F)/t)
   # To reduce underflow, shift/max okay
   s=w.sum()
   if not finite or s<=0: top=(c>=0.99F*?) ; w=...
   w/=s
   # Mix uniform
   w = 0.999*w + 0.001/L
   w /= w.sum()
   return w
```

```
    try: parent = height_sequence_1
    except NameError: return None
    p=np.asarray(parent,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    p=np.clip(p,0,None); s=p.sum()
    if s<=0: return None
    p/=s
    m=p.size
    if m==n: return p
    if m>n:
       # average blocks to n: use contiguous averages
       edges = np.linspace(0,m,n+1).astype(int)
       out = np.array([p[edges[i]:edges[i+1]].mean() if ...])
       # could drop empty, repeat last
    else:
       # upsample linearly
       old = np.linspace(0,1,m) if m>1 else [0]
       new = np.linspace(0,1,n)
       out = np.interp(new, old, p)
    return _normalize(out)
```

Detailed code snippets:

```
def _normalize(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
        a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(a.size, 1.0/a.size)
    a /= s
    return a
```
If a is int list, fine.

`_conv_full`:
```
def _conv_full(p):
    n = p.size
    if n == 0: return np.empty(0)
    if n <= 512:
        c = np.convolve(p, p, mode='full')
    else:
        size = _next_pow2(2*n-1)
        F = np.fft.rfft(p, size)
        c = np.fft.irfft(F*F, size)[:2*n-1]
    return np.maximum(c, 0.0)
```
Using direct for n<=512; for n=512, conv length1023, direct O262k, fine. For many iterations in multiplicative n=512, direct maybe okay? 262k*1000=262M C, maybe okay but FFT faster. Could use FFT threshold 1024. But direct is simpler and accurate. For n=1024 direct 2M; maybe okay but stage mult many; use FFT threshold 256? Let's set direct <=256, else FFT. FFT overhead for n=256 maybe small. Use direct <=256 to avoid roundoff. `size=_next_pow2(2*n-1)`. Note if p has tiny values, c small.

`_H_mv`: direct threshold maybe <=256 too; H via direct convolve with alpha_rev O(3n^2) vs c O(n^2); for n=512 ~1.5M, okay; but many iterations. Use direct <=256, FFT else.
```
def _H_mv(p, alpha):
    n=p.size; L=2*n-1
    rev=alpha[::-1]
    if n <= 256:
       conv=np.convolve(p, rev, mode='full')
    else:
       size=_next_pow2(3*n-2)
       P=np.fft.rfft(p, size)
       A=np.fft.rfft(rev, size)
       conv=np.fft.irfft(P*A, size)
    idx=np.arange(L-1, L-n-1, -1)
    H=conv[idx]
    # tiny imaginary? irfft real
    return H
```
Conv full with direct for n=256 length766, p length256, rev length511: direct O131k, fine.

Multiplicative: Need if `H` contains zeros and lam zero. Use floor.
```
def _multiplicative(p, c, F, rng, end_time, max_iter=1000000):
    n=p.size
    best_p=p.copy(); best_F=F; best_c=c.copy()
    step=0.1; stall=0; tau_fracs=[0.2,0.1,0.05,0.03,0.015,0.008,0.005]
    tau_idx=0; it=0
    while time.monotonic() < end_time:
       it+=1
       alpha=_make_alpha(c, F, F*tau_fracs[tau_idx])
       H=_H_mv(p, alpha)
       support = p > (np.max(p)*1e-12 + 1e-300)
       if not np.any(support): support=np.ones(n, bool)
       lam=float(np.mean(H[support]))
       if not np.isfinite(lam) or lam <= 0:
           # fallback noise
           p = _normalize(p + rng.normal(0,1,n)*1e-4)
           c=_conv_full(p); F=float(c.max()); update? continue
       Hsafe=np.maximum(H, lam*1e-8)
       factor=np.power(lam/Hsafe, step)
       factor=np.clip(factor, 0.02, 50.0)
       p_new = p*factor
       p_new = (1.0 - 1e-5)*p_new + 1e-5/n
       p_new = _normalize(p_new)
       c_new = _conv_full(p_new)
       F_new=float(np.max(c_new))
       if np.isfinite(F_new) and F_new < best_F - 1e-13:
           p, c, F = p_new, c_new, F_new
           best_p,best_F,best_c=p.copy(),F,c.copy()
           stall=0
           if step < 0.5: step *= 1.03
       else:
           if F_new < F: # local current improved but not best? could happen if best_F lower? no best_F<=F; if F_new < F then F_new < best_F? since best_F≤F. So accept. ignore.
           stall += 1
           if stall > 10:
               step *= 0.5
               tau_idx=(tau_idx+1)%len(tau_fracs)
           if step < 0.004:
               # kick
               p = _normalize(p * np.exp(rng.normal(0, 0.05, n)))
               c=_conv_full(p); F=float(np.max(c))
               if F < best_F: update
               step=0.1; stall=0
       if it % 200 == 0 and F != best_F:
           # reset to best to not drift? Maybe current could be on path; keep.
           pass
    return best_p, best_F, best_c
```
If F_new not finite (shouldn't), skip.

`_make_alpha`: if F*tau_frac maybe 0; use absolute `tau`.
```
def _make_alpha(c, F, tau):
    L=c.size
    if F <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(F): return np.full(L,1.0/L)
    t=max(float(tau), F*1e-9)
    w=np.exp((c-F)/t)
    s=w.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0:
       w=(c >= 0.5*F).astype(float)
       s=w.sum()
       if s<=0: w=np.ones(L); s=L
    w/=s
    w = 0.995*w + 0.005/L
    return w/w.sum()
```
If t very small, underflow okay.

`_hankel_matrix`:
```
def _hankel_matrix(alpha, n):
    A=np.empty((n,n), dtype=np.float64)
    for i in range(n):
        A[i, :n] = alpha[i:i+n]
    return A
```
Alpha length 2n-1. Good.

`_hankel`:
```
def _hankel(p,c,F,rng,end_time,max_iter=10000):
    n=p.size
    best...
    beta=0.4
    tau_fracs=[0.5,0.3,0.2,0.15,0.1,0.05,0.03,0.02,0.01]
    idx=0; reg=1e-5; stall=0; it=0
    alpha_prev=None
    while time.monotonic()<end_time:
       it+=1
       alpha=_make_alpha(c,F,F*tau_fracs[idx])
       if alpha_prev is not None:
           alpha = 0.35*alpha + 0.65*alpha_prev
           alpha/=alpha.sum()
       alpha_prev=alpha
       A=_hankel_matrix(alpha,n)
       norm1 = float(np.max(np.abs(A).sum(axis=1))) if n>0 else 1.0
       p_raw=None
       base_reg = max(1e-9, 1e-4*norm1)
       for mult in [1.0,10.0,100.0,1000.0]:
           reg=base_reg*mult
           Areg=A.copy()
           Areg.flat[::n+1] += reg
           try:
               sol=np.linalg.solve(Areg, np.ones(n))
               if np.all(np.isfinite(sol)):
                   p_raw=sol; break
           except np.linalg.LinAlgError:
               pass
       if p_raw is None:
           try:
               p_raw=np.linalg.lstsq(Areg, np.ones(n), rcond=None)[0]
           except:
               p_raw=None
       if p_raw is None:
           p_new=p
           c_new=c; F_new=F
       else:
           pos=np.clip(np.real(p_raw),0,None)
           s=pos.sum()
           if s <= 1e-12:
               p_new=p
               c_new=c; F_new=F
           else:
               p_new=pos/s
               p_new=(1.0-beta)*p + beta*p_new
               p_new=_normalize(p_new)
               c_new=_conv_full(p_new); F_new=float(np.max(c_new))
       if finite and F_new < best_F - 1e-12:
           accept; if F_new < F*0.99? beta=min(1,beta*1.1); else beta*=0.95; stall=0
       else:
           # try direct (beta=1) candidate if pos was valid and not tried?
           if 'direct'?? We only computed mixed. Could evaluate p_pos unscaled maybe too many convs. Skip.
           stall+=1
           if stall%5==0:
               beta *= 0.6
               if beta < 0.05: beta=0.4
               idx=(idx+1)%len(tau_fracs)
           if stall>25:
               # random perturb
               old=...
               p=_normalize(p*np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.05,n)))
               c=conv; F=max
               if not F<best_F: revert
               idx=(idx+1)%len; beta=0.4; stall=0
    return best...
```
Copying A each reg heavy; we can avoid exact by just use base_reg, if solution bad use lstsq with same A (no copy) maybe. Use `Ar = A.copy(); diag add`; if fail, use lstsq(A, b). Simpler.
```
Ar = A.copy(); Ar.flat[::n+1] += base_reg
try solve...
if fail: p_raw = lstsq(A, b) # may be huge but clip
```
If p_raw has negatives many, clip. If no improve, increase base_reg by *=3 for next iter? Keep reg variable.

```
def _coordinate(p,c,F,rng,end_time,max_iter=100000):
   n=p.size
   best...
   fracs = (0.0,0.001,0.01,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5,0.7,0.9,0.999)
   Kactive = min(20, max(1, (2*n-1)//5))
   accepted=0; no_improve=0; it=0
   while time.monotonic()<end_time:
       it+=1
       m=2*n-1
       # choose i,j
       if rng.random() < 0.15:
           # random donor weighted by p
           if p.sum()>0:
              i=int(rng.choice(n,p=p/p.sum()))
           else: i=0
           while True:
              j=int(rng.integers(n))
              if j!=i: break
       else:
           # active k
           if Kactive >= m:
              k=int(rng.integers(m))
           else:
              top=np.argpartition(c, -Kactive)[-Kactive:]
              k=int(top[int(rng.integers(Kactive))])
           lo=max(0,k-(n-1)); hi=min(n-1,k)
           # sample i from [lo,hi] maybe weighted by p_i and p_partner? Use uniform for first
           # To exploit large products, choose weighted by p_i*p_j
           seg_i = np.arange(lo, hi+1)
           weights = np.abs(p[seg_i] * p[k-seg_i])
           sw=weights.sum()
           if sw > 0:
               i=int(seg_i[rng.choice(seg_i.size, p=weights/sw)])
           else:
               i=int(seg_i[rng.integers(seg_i.size)])
           j=k-i
           if i==j:
               r=int(rng.integers(n))
               if r==i: r=(i+1)%n
               i_d=i; j_d=r
           else:
               if p[i] >= p[j]: i_d=i; j_d=j
               else: i_d=j; j_d=i
           i=i_d; j=j_d
       # line search and update
       if i==j or p[i] <= 1e-300:
           no_improve +=1
       else:
           L=np.zeros(m)
           L[i:i+n] -= 2.0*p
           L[j:j+n] += 2.0*p
           q=np.zeros(m)
           q[2*i] += 1.0
           q[2*j] += 1.0
           q[i+j] -= 2.0
           base=p[i]
           best_x=0.0; best_F=F
           # include equalization fraction
           fr = list(fracs) # but creating list each time slow; define global constant _PAIR_FRACTS
           if p[i] > p[j]:
               xe=0.5*(p[i]-p[j])
               if xe > 0 and xe < base: fr.append(xe/base)
           # maybe also fraction p_j/base if <1
           if p[j] > 0 and p[j] < base: fr.append(p[j]/base)
           for frac in fr:
               if frac <=0 or frac >= 1: 
                   if frac == 1: x=base*0.999999? skip
                   continue
               x=base*frac
               val = c + x*L + x*x*q
               mx=float(np.max(val))
               if np.isfinite(mx) and mx < best_F:
                   best_F=mx; best_x=x
           if best_x > 0 and best_F < F - 1e-13:
               c2 = c + best_x*L + best_x*best_x*q
               p2 = p.copy(); p2[i] -= best_x; p2[j] += best_x
               if p2[i] < -1e-15 or p2[j] < -1e-15:
                   # adjust
                   p2 = np.maximum(p2,0); p2=_normalize(p2); c2=_conv_full(p2); best_F=float(np.max(c2))
               else:
                   c2=np.maximum(c2,0.0)
               p,c,F=p2,c2,best_F
               # update best since monotone
               best_p,best_F,best_c=p.copy(),F,c.copy()
               accepted +=1; no_improve=0
               if accepted % 2000 == 0:
                   c=_conv_full(p); F=float(np.max(c))
                   if F < best_F - 1e-12:
                       best_p,best_F,best_c=p.copy(),F,c.copy()
           else:
               no_improve +=1
       if no_improve > 1000:
           # kick
           old... 
           p=_normalize(p + 0.002*rng.normal(0,1,n)); # maybe multiply
           c=_conv_full(p); F=float(np.max(c))
           if F < best_F: update
           else:
               p=best_p.copy(); c=best_c.copy(); F=best_F
           no_improve=0
   return best_p,best_F,best_c
```

`_refine` stage plan:
```
def _refine(p, time_limit, rng):
    p=_normalize(p)
    c=_conv_full(p); F=float(np.max(c))
    n=p.size
    total=time_limit
    now=time.monotonic()
    if total <=0: return p,F,c
    # Build plan
    if n <= 24:
       plan=[('coord',0.45),('hankel',0.35),('mult',0.20)]
    elif n <= 64:
       plan=[('mult',0.15),('hankel',0.45),('coord',0.25),('mult',0.15)]
    elif n <= 256:
       plan=[('mult',0.20),('hankel',0.55),('coord',0.25)]
    elif n <= 512:
       plan=[('mult',0.40),('hankel',0.60)]
    else:
       plan=[('mult',1.0)]
    elapsed=0.0
    for stage, frac in plan:
        stage_time=frac*total
        if stage_time <=0: continue
        end_stage = now + (elapsed + stage_time)
        # check current time maybe if stage_time small due earlier overshoot? Use deadline absolute from now.
        if time.monotonic() >= end_stage - 0.01:
            break
        if stage == 'mult':
            p,F,c = _multiplicative(p,c,F,rng,end_stage)
        elif stage == 'hankel':
            if n <= 512:
                p,F,c = _hankel(p,c,F,rng,end_stage)
        elif stage == 'coord':
            if n <= 256:
                p,F,c = _coordinate(p,c,F,rng,end_stage)
        elapsed += stage_time
        # if no monotone? optimizers return best so p is best
    return p,F,c
```
But elapsed cumulative based on planned total, not actual. If a stage finishes early (no time? optimizers respect end_stage), next end_stage maybe too far elapsed actual less? We use elapsed planned; if stage before finished early, now less than planned end, so next end still okay. If stage overshoots? It shouldn't. But if time.monotonic jumps? okay.
Maybe better `end_stage = end_all - remaining fractions`. Define end_all=now+total; cumulative fraction. `end_stage=now+total*cumfrac`. Good.

In `_refine`, after each stage we should update best? The stage returns best within stage relative to input; if input p at stage start is best from previous, returned p is best over stage (monotone). But if hankel stage no improvement, returns input. So p best. Good.

```
elif len(sel)==3: [0.45,0.3,0.25]
else: [0.35,0.25,0.2,0.2]
```
Use list. Good.

```python
"""
Search for a non-negative step-function height sequence minimizing
    2*n*max(convolve(p,p)) / sum(p)^2.
The code works with sum(p)=1, so the objective is proportional to
max(convolve(p,p)).  It combines several complementary heuristic stages:
  * multiplicative gradient balancing,
  * Hankel-matrix KKT relaxations / non-negative least squares,
  * targeted coordinate pair moves on active convolution lags.
Sequences of many lengths are explored, with the best finite-time candidate
returned.
"""

import numpy as np
import time
import operator

try:
    from scipy.optimize import nnls as _nnls
except Exception:
    _nnls = None

_PAIR_FRACTS = (
    0.001, 0.01, 0.03, 0.08, 0.15, 0.25, 0.40, 0.60, 0.80, 0.95, 0.999
)


def _normalize(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
        a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.full(a.size, 1.0 / a.size, dtype=np.float64)
    a /= s
    return a


def _add_start(starts, p, cap):
    if len(starts) < cap:
        starts.append(_normalize(p))


def _next_pow2(m):
    m = int(m)
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()


def _parent_resample(n, rng):
    try:
        parent = globals().get("height_sequence_1")
    except Exception:
        parent = None
    if parent is None:
        return None
    try:
        p = np.asarray(parent, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    except Exception:
        return None
    if p.size == 0:
        return None
    p = np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    s = float(p.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return None
    p /= s
    m = p.size
    if m == n:
        return p
    if m > n:
        edges = np.linspace(0, m, n + 1).astype(np.int64)
        out = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for i in range(n):
            a = int(edges[i])
            b = int(edges[i + 1])
            if b > a:
                out[i] = float(p[a:b].mean())
            else:
                out[i] = float(p[a]) if a < m else 0.0
        return _normalize(out)
    else:
        if m == 1:
            return _normalize(np.full(n, float(p[0]), dtype=np.float64))
        old_x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, m)
        new_x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
        return _normalize(np.interp(new_x, old_x, p))


def _initial_starts(n, rng, max_starts=16):
    starts = []
    if n <= 0:
        return [np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)]

    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    idx = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)

    # Likely useful skewed starts first.
    for alpha in (1.0, 0.7, 1.4, 0.5, 2.0, 3.0):
        p = np.exp(-alpha * idx / max(1, n))
        _add_start(starts, p, max_starts)
        _add_start(starts, p[::-1], max_starts)

    for a, b in (
        (0.5, 0.0),
        (0.6, 0.15),
        (0.4, -0.15),
        (0.8, 0.0),
        (0.3, 0.0),
        (0.7, -0.2),
    ):
        f = 1.0 + a * np.cos(np.pi * x) + b * np.sin(np.pi * x)
        _add_start(starts, f, max_starts)

    for a in (0.6, 1.0, 1.5, 0.4, 2.0, 3.0):
        base = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        if n > 1:
            base = 1.0 - x
        _add_start(starts, np.maximum(base, 0.0) ** a, max_starts)

    for mu, sigma in ((0.25, 0.2), (0.2, 0.3), (0.35, 0.25), (0.5, 0.35)):
        g = np.exp(-((x - mu) / sigma) ** 2)
        f = 0.75 * g + 0.25
        _add_start(starts, f, max_starts)

    par = _parent_resample(n, rng)
    if par is not None:
        _add_start(starts, par, max_starts)

    # Broader fallbacks.
    _add_start(starts, np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64), max_starts)
    _add_start(starts, 1.0 + 0.15 * rng.random(n), max_starts)

    for _ in range(4):
        f = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for k in range(1, 4):
            amp = rng.uniform(0.05, 0.45) / float(k)
            ph = rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi)
            f = f + amp * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * k * x + ph)
        _add_start(starts, f, max_starts)

    if not starts:
        starts.append(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64))
    return starts


def _score_start(p):
    p = _normalize(p)
    c = _conv_full(p)
    return float(np.max(c)), p


def _conv_full(p):
    n = int(p.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if n <= 256:
        c = np.convolve(p, p, mode="full")
    else:
        size = _next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
        fp = np.fft.rfft(p, size)
        c = np.fft.irfft(fp * fp, size)[: 2 * n - 1]
    return np.maximum(c.astype(np.float64, copy=False), 0.0)


def _make_alpha(c, F, tau):
    L = int(c.size)
    if L <= 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if not np.isfinite(F) or F <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(L, 1.0 / L, dtype=np.float64)
    t = max(float(tau), float(F) * 1e-9)
    w = np.exp((c - F) / t)
    s = float(w.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        w = (c >= 0.5 * F).astype(np.float64)
        s = float(w.sum())
        if s <= 0.0:
            w = np.ones(L, dtype=np.float64)
            s = float(L)
    if s > 0.0:
        w /= s
    # Keep a tiny uniform component for numerical conditioning.
    w = 0.995 * w + 0.005 / L
    w /= w.sum()
    return w


def _H_mv(p, alpha):
    """Return H = Q_alpha p, where (Q_alpha p)_i = sum_j alpha_{i+j} p_j."""
    n = int(p.size)
    L = 2 * n - 1
    if n == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    rev = alpha[::-1]
    if n <= 256:
        conv = np.convolve(p, rev, mode="full")
    else:
        size = _next_pow2(3 * n - 2)
        fp = np.fft.rfft(p, size)
        frev = np.fft.rfft(rev, size)
        conv = np.fft.irfft(fp * frev, size)
    conv = conv.astype(np.float64, copy=False)
    idx = np.arange(L - 1, L - n - 1, -1)
    return conv[idx]


def _multiplicative(p, c, F, rng, end_time, max_iter=1000000):
    n = int(p.size)
    best_p = p.copy()
    best_F = float(F)
    best_c = c.copy()

    step = 0.10
    tau_fracs = (0.25, 0.15, 0.08, 0.05, 0.03, 0.02, 0.012, 0.008)
    tau_idx = 0
    stall = 0
    it = 0

    while time.monotonic() < end_time:
        it += 1
        if it > max_iter:
            break

        alpha = _make_alpha(c, F, F * tau_fracs[tau_idx])
        H = _H_mv(p, alpha)

        pm = float(np.max(p)) if p.size else 0.0
        support = p > (pm * 1e-12 + 1e-300)
        if not np.any(support):
            support = np.ones(n, dtype=bool)

        lam = float(np.mean(H[support]))
        if (not np.isfinite(lam)) or lam <= 0.0:
            old_p, old_c, old_F = p.copy(), c.copy(), float(F)
            p = _normalize(p * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.05, n)))
            c = _conv_full(p)
            F = float(np.max(c))
            if not (np.isfinite(F) and F < best_F):
                p, c, F = old_p, old_c, old_F
            step = 0.10
            stall = 0
            continue

        Hsafe = np.maximum(H, lam * 1e-8)
        factor = np.power(lam / Hsafe, step)
        factor = np.clip(factor, 0.02, 50.0)

        p_new = p * factor
        p_new = (1.0 - 1e-5) * p_new + 1e-5 / n
        p_new = _normalize(p_new)

        c_new = _conv_full(p_new)
        F_new = float(np.max(c_new))

        if np.isfinite(F_new) and F_new < best_F - 1e-13:
            p, c, F = p_new, c_new, F_new
            best_p, best_F, best_c = p.copy(), F, c.copy()
            stall = 0
            if step < 0.5:
                step *= 1.04
        else:
            stall += 1
            if stall > 8:
                step *= 0.5
                tau_idx = (tau_idx + 1) % len(tau_fracs)

            if step < 0.004:
                old_p, old_c, old_F = p.copy(), c.copy(), float(F)
                p = _normalize(p * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.08, n)))
                c = _conv_full(p)
                F = float(np.max(c))
                if not (np.isfinite(F) and F < best_F):
                    p, c, F = old_p, old_c, old_F
                step = 0.10
                stall = 0
                tau_idx = (tau_idx + 1) % len(tau_fracs)

    return best_p, best_F, best_c


def _hankel_matrix(alpha, n):
    A = np.empty((n, n), dtype=np.float64)
    for i in range(n):
        A[i, :n] = alpha[i:i + n]
    return A


def _hankel(p, c, F, rng, end_time, max_iter=100000):
    n = int(p.size)
    best_p = p.copy()
    best_F = float(F)
    best_c = c.copy()

    beta = 0.45
    tau_fracs = (0.50, 0.35, 0.25, 0.18, 0.12, 0.08, 0.05, 0.03, 0.02, 0.012)
    tau_idx = 0
    reg = 1e-5
    stall = 0
    it = 0
    alpha_prev = None

    while time.monotonic() < end_time:
        it += 1
        if it > max_iter:
            break

        alpha = _make_alpha(c, F, F * tau_fracs[tau_idx])
        if alpha_prev is None:
            alpha_cur = alpha
        else:
            alpha_cur = 0.4 * alpha + 0.6 * alpha_prev
            alpha_cur /= alpha_cur.sum()
        alpha_prev = alpha_cur

        A = _hankel_matrix(alpha_cur, n)
        b = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        p_raw = None

        # For small problems, NNLS enforces non-negativity more cleanly.
        if (_nnls is not None) and n <= 96:
            try:
                sol, _ = _nnls(A, b)
                sol = np.asarray(sol, dtype=np.float64)
                if np.all(np.isfinite(sol)) and float(sol.sum()) > 1e-12:
                    p_raw = sol
            except Exception:
                p_raw = None

        if p_raw is None:
            rowsum = float(np.max(np.abs(A).sum(axis=1))) if n > 0 else 1.0
            base_reg = max(1e-9, 1e-4 * rowsum, reg)
            Areg = A.copy()
            Areg.flat[:: n + 1] += base_reg
            solved = False
            try:
                sol = np.linalg.solve(Areg, b)
                if np.all(np.isfinite(sol)):
                    p_raw = sol
                    solved = True
            except Exception:
                solved = False
            if not solved:
                try:
                    sol = np.linalg.lstsq(Areg, b, rcond=None)[0]
                    if np.all(np.isfinite(sol)):
                        p_raw = sol
                except Exception:
                    p_raw = None

        if p_raw is None:
            p_new = p
            c_new = c
            F_new = float(F)
        else:
            pos = np.clip(np.real(p_raw), 0.0, None)
            s = float(pos.sum())
            if s <= 1e-12:
                p_new = p
                c_new = c
                F_new = float(F)
            else:
                p_new = pos / s
                p_new = (1.0 - beta) * p + beta * p_new
                p_new = _normalize(p_new)
                c_new = _conv_full(p_new)
                F_new = float(np.max(c_new))

        if np.isfinite(F_new) and F_new < best_F - 1e-12:
            p, c, F = p_new, c_new, F_new
            best_p, best_F, best_c = p.copy(), F, c.copy()
            stall = 0
            beta = min(1.0, beta * 1.12)
        else:
            stall += 1
            if stall > 4:
                beta *= 0.65
                if beta < 0.05:
                    beta = 0.45
                tau_idx = (tau_idx + 1) % len(tau_fracs)
                reg *= 2.0
                if reg > 5e-2:
                    reg = 5e-2

            if stall > 22:
                old_p, old_c, old_F = p.copy(), c.copy(), float(F)
                p = _normalize(p * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.07, n)))
                c = _conv_full(p)
                F = float(np.max(c))
                if not (np.isfinite(F) and F < best_F):
                    p, c, F = old_p, old_c, old_F
                beta = 0.45
                stall = 0
                tau_idx = (tau_idx + 1) % len(tau_fracs)

    return best_p, best_F, best_c


def _coordinate(p, c, F, rng, end_time, max_iter=1000000):
    n = int(p.size)
    m = 2 * n - 1
    best_p = p.copy()
    best_F = float(F)
    best_c = c.copy()

    full_mode = (n <= 16)
    full_pairs = []
    full_order = None
    full_pos = 0
    if full_mode:
        full_pairs = [(i, j) for i in range(n) for j in range(n) if i != j]
        if full_pairs:
            full_order = rng.permutation(len(full_pairs)).astype(np.int64)

    Kactive = min(20, max(1, m // 5))
    accepted = 0
    no_improve = 0
    it = 0

    while time.monotonic() < end_time:
        it += 1
        if it > max_iter:
            break

        if full_mode and full_pairs:
            idx = int(full_order[full_pos])
            full_pos += 1
            if full_pos >= len(full_pairs):
                full_pos = 0
                full_order = rng.permutation(len(full_pairs)).astype(np.int64)
            i, j = full_pairs[idx]
        elif rng.random() < 0.15:
            ps = float(p.sum())
            if ps > 0.0:
                i = int(rng.choice(n, p=(p / ps).astype(np.float64)))
            else:
                i = int(rng.integers(n))
            if n <= 1:
                no_improve += 1
                continue
            while True:
                j = int(rng.integers(n))
                if j != i:
                    break
        else:
            if Kactive >= m:
                k = int(rng.integers(m))
            else:
                top = np.argpartition(c, -Kactive)[-Kactive:]
                k = int(top[int(rng.integers(Kactive))])

            lo = max(0, k - (n - 1))
            hi = min(n - 1, k)
            seg = np.arange(lo, hi + 1, dtype=np.int64)
            partners = np.array([int(k - int(t)) for t in seg], dtype=np.int64)
            weights = np.abs(p[seg] * p[partners])
            sw = float(weights.sum())
            if sw > 0.0:
                pos = int(rng.choice(seg.size, p=weights / sw))
            else:
                pos = int(rng.integers(seg.size))
            i = int(seg[pos])
            j = int(partners[pos])

            if i == j:
                r = int(rng.integers(n))
                if n == 1:
                    no_improve += 1
                    continue
                if r == i:
                    r = (i + 1) % n
                i, j = i, r
            else:
                if p[i] >= p[j]:
                    i, j = i, j
                else:
                    i, j = j, i

        if i == j or p[i] <= 1e-300:
            no_improve += 1
            continue

        L = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
        L[i:i + n] -= 2.0 * p
        L[j:j + n] += 2.0 * p

        q = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
        q[2 * i] += 1.0
        q[2 * j] += 1.0
        q[i + j] -= 2.0

        base = float(p[i])
        best_x = 0.0
        best_xF = float(np.max(c))

        def eval_frac(fr):
            if fr <= 0.0 or fr >= 0.999999:
                return None
            xx = base * fr
            cand = c + xx * L + xx * xx * q
            val = float(np.max(cand))
            if np.isfinite(val) and val < best_xF:
                return xx, val
            return None

        for fr in _PAIR_FRACTS:
            res = eval_frac(float(fr))
            if res is not None:
                best_x, best_xF = res

        if p[i] > p[j]:
            xe = 0.5 * (p[i] - p[j])
            if xe > 0.0 and xe < base:
                res = eval_frac(float(xe / base))
                if res is not None:
                    best_x, best_xF = res

        if 0.0 < p[j] < base:
            res = eval_frac(float(p[j] / base))
            if res is not None:
                best_x, best_xF = res

        if best_x > 0.0 and best_xF < float(np.max(c)) - 1e-13:
            c2 = c + best_x * L + best_x * best_x * q
            p2 = p.copy()
            p2[i] -= best_x
            p2[j] += best_x
            if p2[i] < 0.0 and p2[i] > -1e-15:
                p2[i] = 0.0
            if p2[j] < 0.0 and p2[j] > -1e-15:
                p2[j] = 0.0

            if np.any(p2 < -1e-12):
                p2 = _normalize(np.maximum(p2, 0.0))
                c2 = _conv_full(p2)
                best_xF = float(np.max(c2))

            c2 = np.maximum(c2, 0.0)
            p, c, F = p2, c2, best_xF
            best_p, best_F, best_c = p.copy(), F, c.copy()
            accepted += 1
            no_improve = 0

            if accepted % 2000 == 0:
                c = _conv_full(p)
                F = float(np.max(c))
                if F < best_F - 1e-12:
                    best_p, best_F, best_c = p.copy(), F, c.copy()
        else:
            no_improve += 1

        if no_improve > 800:
            old_p, old_c, old_F = p.copy(), c.copy(), float(F)
            p = _normalize(p * (1.0 + 0.006 * rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, n)))
            c = _conv_full(p)
            F = float(np.max(c))
            if not (np.isfinite(F) and F < best_F):
                p, c, F = old_p, old_c, old_F
            no_improve = 0

    return best_p, best_F, best_c


def _refine(p, time_limit, rng):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    p = _normalize(p)
    c = _conv_full(p)
    F = float(np.max(c))
    n = int(p.size)

    if not np.isfinite(F):
        p = np.ones(max(1, n), dtype=np.float64)
        p = _normalize(p)
        c = _conv_full(p)
        F = float(np.max(c))

    if time_limit <= 0.0 or p.size <= 0:
        return p, F

    if p.size <= 24:
        plan = (("coord", 0.45), ("hankel", 0.35), ("mult", 0.20))
    elif p.size <= 64:
        plan = (("mult", 0.15), ("hankel", 0.45), ("coord", 0.25), ("mult", 0.15))
    elif p.size <= 256:
        plan = (("mult", 0.20), ("hankel", 0.55), ("coord", 0.25))
    elif p.size <= 512:
        plan = (("mult", 0.35), ("hankel", 0.65))
    else:
        plan = (("mult", 1.0),)

    end_all = time.monotonic() + float(time_limit)
    remaining = float(time_limit)

    for stage, frac in plan:
        if remaining <= 0.05:
            break
        stage_time = min(remaining, float(frac) * float(time_limit))
        if stage_time <= 0.05:
            continue
        end_stage = time.monotonic() + stage_time

        if stage == "mult":
            p, F, c = _multiplicative(p, c, F, rng, end_stage)
        elif stage == "hankel":
            if p.size <= 512:
                p, F, c = _hankel(p, c, F, rng, end_stage)
        elif stage == "coord":
            if p.size <= 256:
                p, F, c = _coordinate(p, c, F, rng, end_stage)

        remaining = end_all - time.monotonic()
        if remaining <= 0.0:
            break

    return p, float(F)


def _update_best_dict(d, n, full_score, seq):
    if not np.isfinite(full_score):
        return
    n = int(n)
    if (n not in d) or (full_score < d[n][0] - 1e-15):
        d[n] = (float(full_score), np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64).copy())


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800.0, **kwargs):
    """Search under the time budget and return the best sequence found."""
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    if budget_s is None:
        budget = 1800.0
    else:
        try:
            budget = float(budget_s)
        except Exception:
            budget = 1800.0
    run_budget = max(60.0, budget - 25.0)
    global_deadline = t0 + run_budget

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    best_score = np.inf
    best_seq = None
    best_by_n = {}

    n_list = [
        8, 12, 16, 24, 32, 48, 64, 96, 128, 192, 256,
        384, 512, 768, 1024, 2048, 4096
    ]

    quick_total = min(420.0, max(90.0, 0.22 * run_budget))
    quick_end = t0 + quick_total

    print(f"start: budget={budget:.1f}s, quick_end={quick_total:.1f}s", flush=True)

    # Quick screening across several lengths.
    for nidx, n in enumerate(n_list):
        if time.monotonic() >= quick_end - 0.2:
            break

        remaining_ns = max(1, len(n_list) - nidx)
        per_N = max(2.5, (quick_end - time.monotonic()) / float(remaining_ns))

        max_starts = 20 if n <= 96 else (12 if n <= 512 else 7)
        starts = _initial_starts(n, rng, max_starts=max_starts)
        scored = []
        for p0 in starts:
            sp, pn = _score_start(p0)
            scored.append((sp, pn))
            full_sp = 2.0 * float(n) * float(sp)
            if np.isfinite(full_sp) and full_sp < best_score:
                best_score = float(full_sp)
                best_seq = pn.copy()
                _update_best_dict(best_by_n, n, full_sp, pn)

        if not scored:
            continue

        scored.sort(key=operator.itemgetter(0))

        if n > 2048:
            keep = 1
        elif n > 1024:
            keep = 2
        else:
            keep = 3
        keep = min(keep, len(scored))

        # Scoring already consumed some time; allocate the rest among kept starts.
        elapsed = time.monotonic() - t0
        allocate_to_refine = max(0.5, per_N - (elapsed - t0 + (nidx * 0.0)) + 0.0)
        # Simpler and safer: use all time left until quick_end, spread over remaining starts.
        total_this_N = max(0.5, quick_end - time.monotonic() - 0.05)
        per_start = max(0.5, total_this_N / float(keep))

        for sp, p0 in scored[:keep]:
            if time.monotonic() >= quick_end - 0.2:
                break
            per_start = min(per_start, max(0.5, quick_end - time.monotonic() - 0.05))
            seq, maxc = _refine(p0, per_start, rng)
            full_score = 2.0 * float(seq.size) * float(maxc)
            _update_best_dict(best_by_n, seq.size, full_score, seq)
            if np.isfinite(full_score) and full_score < best_score:
                best_score = float(full_score)
                best_seq = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64).copy()
                print(f"quick n={seq.size}: score={full_score:.6f}", flush=True)

    # Long refinement of the most promising lengths.
    long_deadline = global_deadline - 5.0
    if time.monotonic() < long_deadline - 20.0 and len(best_by_n) > 0:
        item_list = list(best_by_n.values())
        item_list.sort(key=operator.itemgetter(0))

        selected = []
        for sc, seq in item_list:
            n = int(seq.size)
            already = False
            for _, prev in selected:
                if int(prev.size) == n:
                    already = True
                    break
            if not already:
                selected.append((sc, seq))
            if len(selected) >= 4:
                break

        if not selected:
            selected = [(float(best_score), best_seq)]

        if len(selected) == 1:
            fracs = [1.0]
        elif len(selected) == 2:
            fracs = [0.60, 0.40]
        elif len(selected) == 3:
            fracs = [0.45, 0.30, 0.25]
        else:
            fracs = [0.35, 0.25, 0.20, 0.20]

        long_budget = max(0.0, long_deadline - time.monotonic())
        for k, (sc, seq) in enumerate(selected):
            if time.monotonic() >= long_deadline - 5.0:
                break
            alloc = long_budget * fracs[k]
            alloc = min(alloc, max(0.0, long_deadline - time.monotonic() - 5.0))
            if alloc <= 2.0:
                continue
            print(f"long refine n={seq.size}: alloc={alloc:.1f}s", flush=True)
            new_seq, maxc = _refine(seq, alloc, rng)
            full_score = 2.0 * float(new_seq.size) * float(maxc)
            _update_best_dict(best_by_n, new_seq.size, full_score, new_seq)
            if np.isfinite(full_score) and full_score < best_score:
                best_score = float(full_score)
                best_seq = np.asarray(new_seq, dtype=np.float64).copy()
                print(f"long n={new_seq.size}: score={full_score:.6f}", flush=True)

    # Final short polish of the global best.
    remaining = global_deadline - time.monotonic()
    if best_seq is not None and remaining > 10.0:
        alloc = min(remaining - 3.0, 120.0)
        if alloc > 5.0:
            new_seq, maxc = _refine(best_seq, alloc, rng)
            full_score = 2.0 * float(new_seq.size) * float(maxc)
            if np.isfinite(full_score) and full_score < best_score:
                best_score = float(full_score)
                best_seq = np.asarray(new_seq, dtype=np.float64).copy()

    # Guaranteed fallback.
    if best_seq is None:
        p = np.exp(-np.arange(256, dtype=np.float64) / 256.0)
        best_seq = _normalize(p)
        c = _conv_full(best_seq)
        best_score = 2.0 * float(best_seq.size) * float(np.max(c))

    out = [float(v) for v in best_seq]
    try:
        eval_score = float(evaluate_sequence(out))
    except Exception:
        eval_score = np.inf

    print(f"final internal score={best_score:.6f}, n={len(out)}, "
          f"evaluator score={eval_score:.6f}", flush=True)
    return out
```
